ANNOUNCEMENT BY THE CHAIR 
MR. CHAIRMAN: Hon’ble Members, the Finance Bill, 2026, was included in today’s 
List of Business, subject to the receipt of hon’ble President’s recommendation. The 
same has since been received, and the Bill will now be taken up in the House from 
2.00 P.M. onwards.  
Hon’ble Members, against the allocated seven hours for combined discussion 
on the Appropriation (No.2) Bill, 2026, eight hours and nine minutes have already 
been consumed. In order to allow Members to speak on the Finance Bill, 2026, if the 
House agrees, additional time of three hours may be allocated for the discussion. 
Accordingly, the allocation of time to parties will be made as per 11 hours. 
Thereafter, the hon’ble Finance Minister will give a combined reply to the 
discussions on the Appropriation (No.2) Bill, 2026, and the Finance Bill, 2026.  
 Now, Reports of the Department -Related Parliamentary Standing Committee 
on A griculture, Animal Husbandry and Food Processing, Shrimati Ramilaben 
Becharbhai Bara Ji. 
 
REPORTS OF THE DEPARTMENT-RELATED PARLIAMENTARY STANDING 
COMMITTEE ON AGRICULTURE, ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND FOOD 
PROCESSING 
श्रीमती िगमलाबेन बेचािभाई बािा (गुजरात): महो दय, मैं निभाग -संबंनित क ृ नि, पशुपािि और 
िाद्य प्रसंस्करण संबंिी संसदीय स्थायी सनमनत ( 2025-26) क े  निम्िनिनित प्रनतिेदिों की एक-
एक प्रनत (अंग्रेजी तथा नहन्दी में) सभा पटि पर रिती ह ूँ:-  
(i) Thirty-third Report (Eighteenth Lok Sabha) of the Committee on ‘Production 
and Availability of Oilseeds and Pulses in the Country’ pertaining to the 
Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare (Department of Agriculture  and  
Farmers Welfare);

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

(ii) Thirty-fourth Report (Eighteenth Lok Sabha) of the Committee on ‘Research 
for Developing Water Efficient Variety of Seeds to Save Ground Water’ 
pertaining to the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare (Department of 
Agricultural, Research  and  Education); 
(iii) Thirty-fifth Report (Eighteenth Lok Sabha) of the Committee on A ction Taken 
by the Government on the Observations/Recommendations contained in its 
Seventh Report (Eighteenth Lok Sabha) on ‘Demands for Grants (2025 -26)’ 
pertaining to the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare (Department  of

Demand for conferment of Bharat Ratna on Late Ratan Thiyam (Posthumously) 
SHRI MAHARAJA SANAJAOBA LEISHEMBA (Manipur) : Thank you, Sir, for giving 
me this opportunity. Sir, the subject of my Zero Hour is, ‘Demand for Conferment of 
Bharat Ratna upon late Shri Ratan Thiyam posthumously. I urge the Government to 
confer Bharat Ratna posthumously upon late Shri Ratan Thiyam, one  of India's 
greatest theatre legends and a towering cultural icon from Manipur.  
Shri Ratan Thiyam was a multifaceted genius -- a playwright, director, painter, 
writer and theatre visionary -- who transformed Indian theatre through his unique 
Status of implementation of the Recommendations contained in the Sixth Report of 
the Department-related Parliamentary Standing  Committee on Railways 
THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF RAILWAYS (SHRI RAVNEET 
SINGH): Sir, I lay  a statement regarding status of implementation of the 
Recommendations contained in the Sixth Report (Eighteenth Lok Sabha) of the 
Department-related Parliamentary Standing Committee on Railways on ‘Increasing 
Freight related earning of Indian Railways and Development of Dedicated Freight

MR. CHAIRMAN : Now, ‘Matters raised with Permission.’ Shriman  Maharaja 
Sanajaoba Leishemba. 
 
 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
17

blending of t raditional Manipur aesthetics with modern theatrical expression. Over a 
career spanning more than five decades, he created landmark productions such as 
Chakravyuha, Andha Yug,  Ritu - Samhara  and the Uttar Priyadarshi, etc., which 
brought immense glory to India on the global stage.  He was the first National School 
of Drama (NSD) graduate from Manipur, later serving as its Director from 1997 to 
1999 and as Chairman from 2013 to 2017. In 1976, he founded the ‘Chorus Repertory 
Theatre’ in Imphal, which became  a nationally-acclaimed centre of experimental and 
socially-conscious theatre.  
His contributions were recognized with Padma Shri, Sangeet Natak Akademi 
Award and Sangeet Natak Akademi Fellowship, among many other honours. In 
recognition of his extraordina ry contribution in theatre, he was conferred the ‘Legend 
of India -- Lifetime Achievement Award’ in 2018 by the then Vice -President of India, 
Shri M. Venkaiah Naidu.   Shri Ratan Thiyam elevated Manipuri theatre to national and 
international prominence and became a symbol of India's cultural depth, diversity and 
artistic excellence. I, therefore, urge the Government of India to confer Bharat Ratna 
posthumously upon Shri Ratan Thiyam as a fitting tribute to a legendary son of 
Manipur and one of the greatest cultural ambassadors of India. Thank you, Sir.

श्री िंंजय यादव (नबहार): सभापनत महोदय , आज मैं एक ऐसे मुद्दे की ओर सदि का ध्याि 
आक ृ ष्ट करिा चाहता ह ं, जो क े िि आर्वथक ही िहीं, बल्ल्क कतरव्यबोि, िैनतकता, संिेदिशीिता  
और माििता से जुड़ा हुआ एक भाििात्मक प्रश्ि है। महोदय , हम सब िोगों को याद होगा नक 
प्रिाि मंत्री जी िे  ‘आपदा  में अिसर’ का एक स्िोगि नदया था।   आज िह इतिा घातक रूप िे 
चुका है, उसक े  मैं क ु छ उदाहरण देिा चाहता ह ं।  जब देश पर नकसी प्रकार का संकट आता है या 
18
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

कोई आतंकी हमिा होता है, प्राक ृ नतक आपदा होती है या नकसी प्रकार की दुभाग्यप णर घटिा होती 
है, तो उस समय देश क े  िागनरक पहिे से ही भय, पीड़ा, ददर और असुरक्षा क े  भाि में जी रहे होते 
हैं, िेनकि दुभाग्य से उसी िि क ु छ क ंपनियां, निशेिकर एयरिाइन्स क ं पनियां और क ु छ निजी 
पनरिहि सेिाएं इस संकट में इस आपदा को मुिाफ े  क े  अिसर में तब्दीि कर देती हैं। यह दुि में 
शुद्ध रूप से अिसर तिाशिे की निमरम और निदरयी मािनसकता है। मैं आपका ध्याि पहिगाम की 
आतंकी हमिा की तरफ आक ृ ष्ट करिा चाह ंगा। जब यह हमिा हुआ, उस िि प रा देश इस 
आतंकी घटिा से चचनतत था , सहमा हुआ था, िोग डरे हुए थे , पयरटक िहाूँ से सुरनक्षत बाहर 
निकििा चाहते थे। िेनकि उसी िि निजी एयरिाइन्स क ं पिी िे, जो िोग श्रीिगर से नदल्िी 
आिा चाहते थे, नजसकी 6 हजार से 8 हजार रुपये तक की प्रा इचसग थी, अचािक उसी िि िह 
प्राइचसग 60 हजार से 80 हजार रुपये कर दी। महोदय, नजसिे गोनियों की आिाज सुिी हो , जो 
अपिे बच्चों को िेकर नकसी तरह सुरनक्षत निकििा चाहता हो , यनद उसी िि उसक े  भय का 
दोहि करक े  60 हजार से 80 हजार रुपये का नकराया िस िा जा रहा हो, तब यह आपकी मरी हुई 
िैनतकता का एक प्रत्यक्ष प्रमाण है।  
महोदय, यह क े िि एक घटिा िहीं है, बल्ल्क यह पैटिर बार-बार नदिता है। देश में कोई 
प्राक ृ नतक आपदा हो, बाढ़ हो, सुिाड़ हो,  ऐसी दुघरटिाएं हों या िोग नकसी संकट से निकििा 
चाहते हैं, तब डायिानमक प्राइचसग क े िाम पर नटकट कई गुिा महंगे हो जाते हैं। नजतिा अनिक 
डर, उतिा अनिक दोहि।  बाढ़ में राशि, तेि, पािी और िाद्य पदाथों की कीमतें बढ़ जाती हैं। 
आप Emergency में जाएंगे, तो Ambulence की सर्विस देिे िािे अचािक अपिी कीमतें बढ़ा देते 
हैं और जो App based Cab Services देते हैं, िे भी surge pricing क े  िाम पर 4-5 गुिा अनिक 
नकराया िस िते हैं। मैं सरकार से प छिा चाहता ह ूँ नक क्या यह संिेदिशीि व्यिस्था है ? क्या यह 
माििीय व्यिस्था है? हर िह चीज, जो आपदा में सरकार को मािि को मुफ्त देिी चानहये, यह 
मरी हुई िैनतकत्ता की पराकाष्ठा है। महोदय, आज 146 करोड़ देशिानसयों क े  सामिे सबसे बड़ा 
सिाि सरकार की भ नमका को िेकर है नक इि सब मौकों पर सरकार कहाूँ है ? उसका स्टैंड क्या 
है?  
महोदय, यह समस्या क ेिि आपदा तक ही सीनमत िहीं है, दुभाग्य से यह मािनसकता अब 
भारत की रोजमरा की व्यिस्था में भी दीमक की तरह घुस चुकी है। जब देश संकट में हो , िोग डरे 
हुए हों, तब िाद्य पदाथों क े  दाम बेतहाशा बढ़ जाते हैं। जमािोरी, मुिाफािोरी, कािाबाजारी बढ़ 
जाती है और व्यापारी स्टॉक कर मुिाफा कमािे में िग जाते हैं। सभापनत महोदय, अगर नकसी की

Need to address digital addiction and youth mental health 
SHRI DEREK O’BRIEN (West Bengal): Hon’ble Chairman, Sir, studies are showing 
that children and youth are spending up to eight hours a day on screens and their 
mobile phones, that means more than 100 days every year. In fact, 68 countries in the 
world have stopped mobile phones fro m entering schools. Because once this 
happens, what happens? Sleep patterns change, there is a risk of anxiety and mood 
swings. Sadly, annually, about 20,000 children are committing suicide because of this 
digital addiction. Actually, what happens is that there is a dopamine spike. Dopamine 
spike means the brain receives a rapid reward signal, which reinforces the behaviour 
and this produces what is known as digital addiction. But, I do not want to only state 
the problem. Here are some suggestions for youth  as well as for adults. One: When 
you go to pick up your phone, ask yourself, ‘Why are you picking up your phone?’ 
And experts say, one trick is, you count up to 10 before you touch your phone. One, 
two, three, four, five, -- you count up to 10 -- then only you pick up your phone. The 
second suggestion, people say, is: When you are charging your phone, keep it in 
another room. The third suggestion is: Two hours before you go to sleep, do not 
touch your mobile phone. And, the fourth one is: What is termed as  the sound of 
silence -- no screen time, no phone time. But, this is difficult to achieve because, 
sometimes, when you are looking at the screen, your brain will get scrambled *.                                                      
That is one example.  It is happening now.

Now, Shri Subhasish Khuntia. 
Need for restoration of dilapidated Nilachal Ashok Hotel in Puri 
SHRI SUBHASISH KHUNTIA (Odisha) : Jai Jagannath!  Thank you, Mr. Chairman, 
Sir, for giving me the opportunity to draw your kind attention to an urgent matter 
regarding the neglected and dilapi dated condition of Nilachal Ashok Hotel in Puri, 
Odisha, which was once a prominent hotel under the Indian Tourism Development 
Corporation. The hotel is located at a prime sea-facing site in the holy city of Puri, one 
of India's revered Char Dham pilgrimage centres.  It used to once serve as a landmark 
destination for both domestic and international tourists. With lakhs of devotees visiting 
Puri every day, it continues to be one of the most significant religious tourism hubs in 
the country. Puri is a part o f major tourist circuit located near renowned destination 
like UNESCO Heritage Konark Sun Temple and Asia's biggest Chilka Lake which is 55 
24
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

kilometres away, which enhances its importance as a key tourist destination hub.  
However, it is deeply concerning t hat in recent years, the Nilachal Ashok Hotel has 
been abandoned and left in a state of severe neglect. The once prestigious property 
now resembles a deteriorating structure adversely affecting the image of Puri as a 
prime tourist destination.   
Sir, at a time when millions of pilgrims and tourists visit Puri annually, the 
demand for quality accommodation and tourism infrastructure remains very high. It is 
indeed unfortunate that such a valuable Government asset has been allowed to 
decay. It is al so pertinent to mention that the present sitting Member of Lok Sabha 
from Puri had earlier served as the Chairman of the ITDC.  There was a reasonable 
expectation that during his tenure, necessary steps would be taken to make the hotel 
operational.  However, it is matter of regret that the issue did not receive the required 
attention.   
In this context, the vision of ‘Sabka Saath, Sabka Vikas’  calls for inclusive and 
balanced development of all regions and sector, including vital to tourism 
infrastructure i n culturally -significant cities like Puri. The present condition of this 
property stands in contrast to that broader development vision. I humbly urge the 
Union Minister of Tourism to take immediate and appropriate action for the restoration 
and revival of  Nilachal Ashok Hotel at Puri.  Reviving this property will not only 
strengthen tourism infrastructure in Puri but also restore pride in the city, rich heritage 
and hospitality tradition. Jai Jagannath; Bandhe Utkal Janani!

श्री निहिी अमीन (गुजरात): माििीय सभापनत महोदय, माििीय प्रिाि मंत्री, श्री िरेन्द्र मोदी 
जी की सरकार में 2026 -27 में रेििे क े  बजट में 2.93 िाि करोड़ रुपए का प्राििाि नकया गया 
है। कांग्रेस क े  शासि में 2013-14 में रेििे मंत्रािय का बजट 63,363 करोड़ रुपए था। 2013 -14 क े  
बजट से इस साि का बजट चार गुिा ज्यादा है। भारत सरकार िे इस साि गुजरात राज्य को 
17,366 करोड़ रुपए का बजट नदया है। अमृत भारत योजिा क े  तहत गुजरात में 87 से ज्यादा 
स्टेशिों को अपग्रेडेशि क े  निए पसंद नकया गया है। इिमें से 19 स्टेशिों का काम प रा हुआ है और 
बाकी स्टेशिों क े  अपग्रेडेशि का काम प्रगनत में है।  
 महोदय, अहमदाबाद शहर की जिसंख्या िगभग 80 िाि से ज्यादा है। अहमदाबाद 
रेििे स्टेशि, जो सबसे बड़ा रेििे स्टेशि है और शहर क े  बीच में है, उस स्टेशि क े  आसपास 
ज्यादा जिसंख्या  रहती है और िहां पर रैनफक की भी बड़ी समस्या है। इसक े  अिािा, 
अहमदाबाद में दो और रेििे स्टेशंस हैं - साबरमती और असारिा, जो पल्श्चम अहमदाबाद से द र 
हैं। पल्श्चम अहमदाबाद में 20 िाि से ज्यादा िोग रहते हैं। इस इिाक े  में रहिे िािे िोगों को 
अहमदाबाद और साबरमती स्टेशंस आिे-जािे में िंबा समय िगता है। पल्श्चम अहमदाबाद एनरया

दो नमिट का  स्टॉ पेज नदया  जाए, तो बहुत से िोगों को बड़ी राहत होगी , िोगों को ज्यादा द र 
िहीं जािा पड़ेगा और बाकी स्टेशिों पर िोड भी कम रहेगा।

करोड़ है। Population density की बात करें, तो आज भारत में एक िगर नकिोमीटर में 481 िोग

किाडा में मात्र 4 िोग रहते हैं। इतिे कम भ -भाग पर इतिी बड़ी आबादी का दबाि हमारे 
प्राक ृ नतक और आर्वथक संसाििों को िुकसाि पहुंचा रहा है। 
महोदय, इस population explosion क े  कारण हमारी सरकार की बेहतरीि निकास 
योजिाओं का प रा िाभ आम जिता तक िहीं पहुूँ च पा रहा है। हमारी सरकार िे नपछिे 10 सािों 
में नरकॉडर अस्पताि , स्क  ि और सड़क ें  बिाई हैं, िेनकि बेतहाशा बढ़ती जिसंख्या क े  कारण ये 
सारी सुनििाएं हमेशा कम पड़ जाती हैं। हमारे शहर अपिी क्षमता से बाहर जा चुक े  हैं। 2050 तक 
हम 165 करोड़ क े  आंकड़े को भी पार कर सकते हैं। आदरणीय प्रिाि मंत्री, श्री िरेन्द्र मोदी जी िे 
2019 में िाि नकिे से नबल्क ु ि सही कहा था, "छोटा पनरिार रििा भी देशभनि की एक निशािी

Demand for development of Beypore Port in Kerala 
SHRIMATI P. T. USHA (Nominated): Respected Chairman, Sir, I rise to bring to the 
urgent attention of this august House a matter of immense significance for the State of 
Kerala and for the broader maritime development of our nation - the accelerated 
development of Beypor e Port through capital dredging and associated infrastructure 
expansion.  
Beypore is an important Non -Major Port on the Malabar coast, and its 
potential remains vastly untapped. The proposed capital dredging, which aims to 
deepen the existing waterway and remove accumulated material from the seabed, is 
essential to enable the passage and berthing of larger vessels. This single intervention 
can dramatically expand the cargo and passenger handling capacity of the port.  
Such development will not only benefit Kerala but also contribute meaningfully to 
India's maritime sector. The Malabar region, in particular, stands to gain through 
direct and indirect employment opportunities, boosting shipping, logistics, tourism, 
and allied industries. Enhancing the port's i nfrastructure by increasing the length of 
the existing wharf, undertaking capital dredging, and acquiring land for the 
construction of warehouses and cargo -handling facilities will, significantly, improve 
operational efficiency and economic output.  
I woul d also like to highlight that apart from Kochi and Thiruvananthapuram, 
Kozhikode is a major domestic and international tourist destination. The development 
of Beypore Port will serve as a strategic catalyst to boost tourism and associated 
economic activiti es in the region. Sir, this is not merely a regional aspiration; it is a 
national opportunity. I, therefore, urge the Central Government to take immediate 
steps to fast-track the Beypore Port development project and ensure its time -bound 
36
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

completion, so that Kerala and the nation can fully realise the economic and strategic 
advantages this port can offer. Thank you, Sir.

उसको integrate करिा चानहए। जब भी  unconscious patients हॉल्स्पटि में इमरजेंसी िॉडर में 
पहुंचते हैं, तो उस टाइम जो भी डॉक्टर होता है, िह उिको देिता है, उसक े  पास इि मरीजों की 
कोई नडटेि िहीं होती है।  ‘आभा’ (आयुष्माि भारत हेल्थ अकाउंट) एक नसस्टम है, उसमें digital 
data होता है। उसको यनद ड्राइचिग िाइसेंस क े  साथ emergency medical QR code क े  माध्यम 
से integrate नकया जाए , ड्राइचिग िाइसेंस क े  अंदर QR code डािा जाए और हेिमेट में भी 
डािा जाए, तो ऐसी क ं डीशि में यह simple scan डॉक्टसर क े  निए essential time बचाता है और 
िे नसफ र  उसको स्क ै ि करिे से उस मरीज की प री जािकारी जैसे उसका क्या मेनडकि रीटमेंट 
चि रहा है , क्या नकसी ड्रग से  allergy है या उसको  diabetes, hypertension या नकसी और 
चीज की कोई  history है नक िहीं , उसक े immediately पता चि जाएगा और साथ में  blood 
group भी पता चि जाएगा। ऐसे समय में  together, all these measures, can convert 
millions of citizens into life savers and significantly strengthen India’s emergency 
response system.  
महोदय, ultimately, गििरमेंट से मेरी नडमांड है नक इि चीजों पर ध्याि नदया जाए , तो 
आज 1 िाि 77 हजार िोगों की जो जाि चिी गई है, उसमें काफी नगरािट या कमी िा सकते हैं। 
आपिे मुझे इस नििय पर बोििे क े  निए समय नदया, उसक े  निए मैं िन्यिाद करता ह ं।

ORAL ANSWERS TO QUESTIONS 
Encroachment on Railways land 
*361. SHRI ANIL KUMAR YADAV MANDADI : Will the Minister of RAILWAYS be 
pleased to state: 
(a) whether Government is maintaining complete and updated database 
pertaining to extent of land possessed by Railways;  
(b) if so, the details thereof;  
(c) whether it is a fact that certain extent of land possessed by Railways is under 
encroachment;  
(d) if so, the details thereof;  
(e) whether any steps have been taken to retrieve the encroached railway 
land/property; and  
(f) if so, the details thereof?  
THE MINISTER OF R AILWAYS (SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW): (a) to (f) A Statement 
is laid on the Table of the House. 
Statement 
(a) to (f) Regular inspections, demarcations and periodical verification of the 
railway land boundaries are carried out by railway administration and accordingly land 
records are updated. 
Indian Railway works closely with state revenue authorities to ensure 
continuous verification and updation of land records wherever required. 
As on 01.04.2025, Indian Railway owns approximately 4.99 lakh hectares of 
land, out of which about 0.21% (1068 ha) of Railway land is under encroachment. 
Regular field surveys are carried out by railways and necessary action is taken 
to remove the encroachment with the assistance of state governments. 
During the last five years, about 98.02 Ha of railway land has been reclaimed 
from encroachments. 
40
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

The vacant railway land is utilized for infrastructural development viz. Multi 
Tracking, Railway Workshops, New Passenger Terminals, Cargo Terminals, etc., as 
per opera tional needs. Land, which is not immediately required by Railways, is 
entrusted to Rail Land Development Authority (RLDA) for commercial utilisation.

DR. K. LAXMAN: Sir, at the outset, I welcome the Ministry of Railways for taking up

[ 27 March, 2026 ]
43

routes, and they are minting money like anything. Sir, it has become a risk for train

SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW : Mr. Chairman, Sir, hon’ble senior Member, Dr. 
Lakshman, has asked a very valid point. We are using two basic technologies, one is 
satellite technology and second is the drone -based survey. And, both these are 
producing maps, which are then compared with the reference maps that we have for 
the entire network. And, based on those reference maps, we are able to use AI and 
other algorithms to find out if any new encroachments have happ ened. And, then, we 
are able to take immediate steps. That is why, practically, fresh encroachments have 
significantly reduced or almost stopped. That is the situation. The problem is more of 
the encroachments which have been there for many decades now. Th at is one part.  
Coming to the second part of the question regarding the MMTS corridor, I would like 
to request the hon’ble Member that I can get the exact data of the corridors that he 
has asked and then share it with him.

MR. CHAIRMAN: Hon’ble Minister.  
SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW : Sir, the unused lands which the hon’ble Member has 
pointed out, I would like to say, we have created a very transparent mechanism and 
44
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

the results are visible. Over the last few years, we have been able to create a very 
transparent mechanism. We tried it i n multiple ways. Railways has an Authority called 
RLDA, the Railway Land Development Authority. That Authority was created many 
years ago. But somehow, in between, during the UPA Government, many steps were 
taken which were not very transparent. So, practically, that work almost got stopped. 
But, in the recent times, very transparent methods have been created by which 
today, in a very authentic tender -based mechanism, the lands are then used for 
commercial development or housing development. So, those proje cts have come out 
very well. There is one project in Ludhiana -- I can share with the hon’ble Member -- 
where a very large multi -storey building has been constructed on the unused land. 
There are similar examples in Bengaluru, in Chennai, and another examp le is recently 
coming up in the New Delhi area; there are examples in Bhubaneswar also. In so 
many cities, the unused land is now very transparently auctioned, rather developed, 
in which there is a revenue -sharing mechanism, disco vered through a tender 
process. And, in the year 2025 -26, that is, the current financial year, we are 
expecting about 900 crore rupees of revenue out of that. And, this is going to 
increase significantly because of the way the programme has been structured. But, 
as I pointed out ri ght in the beginning, hon’ble Chairman, Sir, most of the land of 
railways, the numbers look very large -- about 80 per cent is along the tracks and 
about 5 per cent is that safety margin. And, after that, the land which is left, which is 
a very large numbe r, is the land which has been left for expansion of the railway 
network. And, as you would have noticed, in the last many years, many new 
doubling, quadrupling, third line projects have been sanctioned by the 
Government.That is one of the reasons why today  Railway is able to cater to so many 
passengers, especially, the people of low income and middle income families. So, 
today, we are able to run about 80,000 special trains and that is because we have 
been able to increase the capacity.   That is the though t process that we should try 
and preserve these lands and use that for expanding the railway network.

[ 27 March, 2026 ]
45

hands-on training and fieldlevel gu idance to farmers of the region, as 
many farmers are unable to travel to its Hyderabad office;  
(b) if not, the reasons therefor;

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FARMERS WELFARE (SHRI SHIVRAJ 
SINGH CHOUHAN): (a) to (d) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
Statement 
(a)  and (b) ICAR- Central Research Institute for Dryland Agriculture (CRIDA) 
is conducting basic and strategic research on dryland agriculture addressing the 
problems of dryland and rainfed regions of the country including Nanded.  Besides, 
CRIDA through All India Coo rdinated Research Project on Dry Land Agriculture 
(AICRPDA) and All India Coordinated Research Project on Agro -Meteorology 
(AICRPAM) centres and National Innovations in Climate Resilient Agriculture - 
Technology demonstration component (NICRA-TDC) centres located in the region is 
working to provide hands -on training and field level guidance to farmers of the 
Nanded region to avoid unnecessary travel of farmers to Hyderabad office.  
 The AICRPAM and AICRPDA centre at Parbhani are undertaking critical and 
strategic research, focusing on the evaluation and establishment of region -specific 
crops and cropping systems, rainwater management, nutrient management, energy 
management, alternative land use management, Rainfed Integrated Farming 
Systems, resource charact erization, agro advisories and the establishment of crop -
weather-insect-pest relationships for major crops of dryland agriculture. The study 
findings of the Marathwada Region including Nanded are communicated to farmers 
through centres of NICRA -TDC and the Krishi Vigyan Kendras (KVKs) located in the 
Marathwada region.  
 The Marathwada area is home to Vasantrao Naik Marathwada Krishi 
Vidyapeeth in Parbhani, which has been operational since 1972, focusing on 
research, education, training, and the extension of  agriculture tailored to regional 
needs. The university has the AICRPAM and AICRPDA center at Parbhani to cater the 
46
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

needs of the entire agroclimatic region of Central Maharashtra Zone of Maharashtra 
comprising of the districts of Aurangabad, Parbhani, Hing oli, Nanded, Beed, Latur, 
Osmanabad, and Jalna. Krishi Vigyan Kendra, Limbgaon and Nanded is providing 
expert guidance on crop production, soil health, pest control, and modern dryland 
agricultural techniques. 
 Further, district -level contingency plans to address climatic aberration and 
effect of extreme weather events have been developed and disseminated among 
various stakeholders at the grassroots level. ICAR -CRIDA is consistently holding pre -
kharif interface meetings with state authorities to solicit sug gestions and engage in 
discussions about the district -level contingency plan and shared with the state 
department officials for implementation. Besides ICAR -CRIDA, several other ICAR 
institutes (Annexure-I) and state agricultural universities continues to provide training 
and field level guidance to farmers of the region.   
 The AICRPDA, AICRPAM and KVKs (NICRA -TDC), located in Marathwada 
region, are working as a camp office of the CRIDA to provide hands -on training and 
field level guidance to farmers of Nanded region. 
 (c) A total of 86 technologies/products/practices/methodologies were 
developed by CRIDA and its AICRP Centres suitable for different agro -ecologies of 
country including Marathwada region of Maharashtra during last three years. ICAR-
CRIDA has developed 20 technologies/practices ( Annexure-II), AICRPAM network 
have developed 5 new technologies ( Annexure-III), AICRPDA network have 
developed new 44 technologies/ practices ( Annexure-IV) and NICRA network has 
developed 17 new technologies/ practices (Annexure-V). 
 (d) The outcome of the Institute has also been evaluated by an independent 
Third Party every five years. In 2021, the third -party assessment was done by 
Administrative Staff College of India (ASCI) and ranked “Outstanding”. Recently (in 
January, 2026) the outcomes of the AICRPDA/AICRPAM/CRIDA/NICRA network 
have been assessed by the Quality Council of India (QCI). The impact and field level 
outcomes of these technologies is monitored and evaluated by Quinquennial Review 
Team eve ry five -year, Research Advisory Committee and Institute Management 
Committee every year by a panel of experts constituted by Secretary, Department of 
Agricultural Research and Education (DARE)  and  DG, ICAR. A high-level monitoring 
committee under the cha irmanship of Secretary, DARE and DG, ICAR with invited 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
47

members representing different ministries also review the NICRA and other network 
projects.

Annexure-III 
Technologies generated at AICRPAM during last three years 
1. Developed ‘Criteria -based decisions for Agroclimatic onset of crop season’ 
based on rainfall and soil moisture dynamics. Developed district -level onset 
dates, will ensure better crop establishment compared to conventional so wing 
strategy based on the onset of monsoon especially during the kharif season.  
2. Micro-level Agromet Advisory Services (MAAS)  conceptualized and 
implemented to enhance the accuracy and operational feasibility of block level 
agromet advisories. The program  was implemented in 50 selected villages 
across 20 states of the country covering 1250 farmers.  
3. Agroclimatic Atlases were developed using long term block -level daily climate 
data which is useful for climate-based crop planning, seasonal monitoring, and 
adaptive farming, and supports real-time agromet advisories. 
4. “Weather Cock” Software was developed for the analysis of agroclimatic data 
uniformly aid in agrometeorological analysis and decision support. It supports 
creation of agroclimatic atlases and distr ict profiles in a standardized format, 
enabling better comparison and national-level integration

initiatives undertaken in this regard? 
THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FARMERS WELFARE (SHRI SHIVRAJ 
SINGH CHOUHAN): (a) to (c) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
Statement 
(a) to (c)   The Department of Agriculture  and   Farmers Welfare (DA and FW) 
is implementing various schemes to promote Crop diversification including the 
National Food Security  and  Nutrition Mission (NFSNM) under which, Millet (Shree 
Anna) cultivation is promoted. Interventions under NFSNM are being  implemented 
across 28 States and 2 Union Territories (UTs) including Punjab, for enhancing the 
production of various crops including Millets (Shree Anna) through area expansion 
and productivity improvement. Under NFSNM, for the financial year 2025 -26, an 
Annual Action Plan of Rs. 34.60 lakhs (Central share +state share) was approved for 
the state of Punjab to promote Millet cultivationin the state. The Approved Plan for 
NFSNM -Shree Anna includes assistance to the farmers through the state for 
demonstration, seed distribution, seed production, INM and IPM distribution,  
training etc. 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
61

Further, the Crop Diversification Programme (CDP) under the Pradhan Mantri - 
Rashtriya Krishi Vikas Yojana (PM -RKVY) is also being implemented in the Original 
Green Revolution States viz; Haryana, Punjab and Uttar Pradesh since 2013 -14 to 
divert the area of water guzzling paddy crop to alternative crops like millets, pulses, 
oilseeds, coarse cereals etc. In 2025 -26, an Annual Action Plan of Rs. 103.75cr 
(central share + state sh are) has been approved for the state of Punjab for various 
interventions supportingcrop diversification from Paddy to other alternative crops. 
 The Indian Council of Agriculture Researchensures the production of Breeder 
Seeds of new varieties/hybrids of mi llets. A total of 110 varieties of millets have been 
released during the last three years. High -yielding, climate -resilient, bio -fortified 
varieties or hybrids that are resistant to insects, pests and diseases, as well as of 
short and medium duration (less  than 10 years old) that  outperform existing 
prominent varieties are promoted under NFSNM. In Punjab, a total of 140 quintals of 
certified seed of millets have been distributed under the scheme duringfinancial year 
2025-26.  
The Government has been taking various initiatives to promote millet 
cultivation, processing and consumption across the country alongwith incentivizing 
food processing units, and exporters engaged in millet -based products. The Ministry 
of Food Processing Industries (MoFPI) is implementi ng the Production Linked 
Incentive Scheme for the Food Processing Industry (PLISFPI) from 2021 -22 to 2026-
27 and one component of the scheme focuses on Millet -Based Products, with an 
outlay of Rs. 800 crores (PLISMBP). Under the scheme, 29 applicants have been 
incentivized with an amount of Rs. 793.27 crore in the country including three projects 
in Kapurthala, Patiala and Jalandhar districts of Punjab. 
 Further, under the Pradhan MantriFormalisation of Micro Food Processing 
Enterprises (PMFME) Scheme, Mill ets and millet -based products have been 
identified as One District One Product (ODOP) in 21 districts of 11 States and 17 
Incubation Centers have been approved in 10 States having Millet processing lines. 
Under PMFME, 4,612micro food processing enterprises  have been approved with 
subsidy of Rs.91.20 Crores for millet processing unitsacross the country. In Punjab, 5 
micro food enterprises for millet units have been approved subsidy amounting to Rs. 
0.25 croreunder the scheme. 
 The Indian Council of Agriculture Research – Indian Institute of Millets 
Research (ICAR -IIMR) Hyderabad, through its Centre of Excellence (CoE) and 
62
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

"Nutrihub" technology business incubator, have developed diverse portfolio of value-
added technologies inclu ding Ready -to-Eat (RTE) and Ready -to-Cook (RTC) 
products (e.g., millet pasta, vermicelli, cookies, puffs, and flakes). These 
processing technologies are available for licensing to startups, Farmer Producer 
Organizations (FPOs), and entrepreneurs from acros s the country including Punjab. 
Farmers from Punjab actively participated in the training programs on 
"Entrepreneurship development on millet value added products and marketing" 
organized by IIMR's Nutrihub in early 2019, which aimed to create awareness an d 
provide training on startup and business opportunities in the millet sector. The ICAR -
IIMR, Hyderabad have also signed Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) with 
Punjab-based entities for promoting value -added millet products and for supporting 
initiatives with technical backstopping for millet promotion. 
 In addition, the Punjab Government have informed that the state is organizing 
exhibitions, fairs, state, district and village level camps, talks in schools and colleges 
to create awareness among the consume rs and farmers about the health benefits of 
millets. The state is also promoting value added products of millets to create better 
income opportunity for farmers. The state Government has informed that they have 
initiated a state project “MERA” (Millets for Encouraging Soil Restoration and Healthy 
Food Accessibility)for boosting farmers income and consumer health. The main 
components of the “MERA” are production, promotion, processing, marketing and 
consumption of organic millets.Further, the Department of F ood Science and 
Technology, Punjab Agriculture University, Ludhiana has developed dual purpose 
varieties of bajra and sorghum with good popping potential and high nutrition value 
and these are being promoted among farmers.

आस-पास क े  सारे गाूँि में यह प्रयोग फ ै ि रहा है। इसनिए चाहे परािी प्रबंिि का काम हो, चाहे 
िई पद्धनतयाूँ अपिािे का काम हो , चाहे जमीि की चचता हो -  पंजाब का नकसाि यह भी निरंतर 
कर रहा है। जहाूँ  तक माििीय संि  जी िे सिाि प छा है , उन्होंिे इकोिॉनमक्स का एक सिाि 
कहा है नक िे नमिेट, व्हीट और पैडी का मुकाबिा िहीं कर पा रहे हैं, तो मैं उन्हें बड़ी नििम्रता क े

श्री िंभापगत: क्िेश्चि िम्बर 364. 
Integration of digital land records with agricultural schemes 
*364. SHRIMATI SULATA DEO: Will the Minister of AGRICULTURE AND FARMERS 
WELFARE be pleased to state: 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
69

(a)  whether the Ministry proposes to introduce integration of digital land records 
with agricultural schemes in Odisha to ensure transparency in Direct Benefit 
Transfer (DBT); and 
(b)  if so, the details thereof and if not, the reasons therefor? 
THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FARMERS WELFARE (SHRI SHIVRAJ 
SINGH CHOUHAN): (a) and (b) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
Statement 
 (a) and (b) Yes, Odisha is utilizing digital land records of farmers for the 
purpose of Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT) under various agricultural schemes.  Odisha 
has operationalized the integration of digital land records, i.e., the Bhulekh database, 
with agricultural schemes in the State. The land records in the State are digitized and 
maintained by the Revenue and Disaster Management Department, Govt. of Odisha, 
in the Bhulekh portal. 
The State of Odisha has informed that the digitized land record database of the 
State is used for identification of landholding categories (such as small and marginal 
farmers) under the State Sector farmers’ welfare scheme, i.e. CM KISAN.  
Further, the State Farmers Registry under AgriStack, built using the Record of 
Rights in the sta te of Odisha covers landholding farmers, livestock and fisheries 
farmer, and provides them with a digital identity, called Farmer ID. It has been 
designed to integrate and validate farmer -related data. This helps enable accurate 
identification of eligible beneficiaries and helps enable Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT) 
to the farmers with enhanced transparency, and efficient delivery of scheme benefits. 
Odisha has linked the AgriStack to register new farmers under the PM KISAN 
scheme. 
Under both CM KISAN and PM  KISAN schemes, financial assistance is 
transferred directly to the bank accounts of eligible farmers through DBT mode. 
 The State also captures digitized land record information at the time of farmer 
registration / Krushak Odisha (the State’s own farmer database) at the time of farmer 
registration. These records are subsequently used for DBT of various benefits, 
including seed input subsidy and need -based input assistance, to the farmers across 
the State.During the release of 22nd installment of PM Kisan i n Odisha, 34,18,193 
beneficiaries received benefit of scheme amounting to 683.64 cr. The total amount 
70
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

disbursed till date through DBT for the farmers in Odisha under PM -KISAN is 
Rs.14,486.34 crores. 
Further, the Digital Crop Survey, i.e., survey to digital ly capture crops being 
grown on every farmland in the country with photos and geo -tags, has enabled plot-
level visibility of crops and better estimation of areas sown across seasons, which in 
turn supports evidence-based planning for production estimation, procurement, input 
supply and logistics management. In Kharif 2025, the Digital Crop Survey (DCS) has 
been conducted in 604 Districts covering more than 28.5 crores plots, including 2.56 
crores plots in Odisha.   
MR. CHAIRMAN: First supplementary; Shrimati Sulata Deo.

में कम हुई है। इसका मतिब यह हुआ नक बड़े िोगों से जो पैसे आिे थे, िे year-to-year base में

सर, इस फाइिेंस नबि में 2047 तक 'नगफ्ट नसटी' को नरयायत दी गई है। उस 'नगफ्ट

यह सरकार उिको नगफ्ट दे रही है , उिको नरयायत दे रही है। आपको नकसाि िहीं नदि रहा है , 
महूँगाई से परेशाि िह चहदुस्तािी िहीं नदि रहा है। मुझे कहते हुए दुि होता  है नक 232 पेज क े  
इस फाइिेंस नबि में एक जगह भी मनहिा क े  निए क ु छ भी िहीं है। मनहिा नित्त मंत्री होते हुए भी

िोग हैं। *  
 सर, गुजरात महात्मा गाूँिी जी की जन्म भ नम है।  नकसी और का िहीं , गुजरात गाूँिी का

सेंटर िगाएूँगे, तो उिको 2047 तक टैक्स में नरयायत दी जाएगी।  यह फाइिेंस नबि कहता है।

in 2023 -24, and Rs. 2,17,165 crore in 2024 -25, totalling Rs. 5,99,567 crore in tax 
revenues from the State of Tamil Nadu over the last thre e years. However, Sir, the 
funds devolved to Tamil Nadu from the share of Central taxes during the same period 
were Rs. 38,731 crore in 2022 -23, Rs. 46,072 crore in 2023-24, and Rs. 52,492 crore 
in 2024-25, totalling Rs. 1,37,295 crore.  For example, for e very Rs. 1 of tax revenue 
collected by the Union Government from Tamil Nadu, the State receives only about 23 
paise as its share from the Central divisible pool of taxes. This is nothing short of fiscal 
injustice. A comparison with other States reveals the magnitude of this imbalance. For 
example, Uttar Pradesh contributed Rs. 3,62,457 crore in taxes to the Union 
Government over the same three -year period. Yet, the funds devolved to Uttar 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
93

Pradesh from Central taxes amounted to Rs. 6,03,220 crore. This means  that for 
every Rs. 1 of tax revenue collected by the Union Government from Uttar Pradesh, it 
receives about Rs. 1.66 back from the share of Central taxes. The disparity becomes 
even more striking in the case of Bihar. The taxes collected from Bihar during  these 
three years totalled to Rs. 49,215 crore, while the funds devolved to the State from 
Central taxes amounted to Rs. 3,38,549 crore. In effect, for every Rs. 1 of tax revenue 
collected by the Union Government from Bihar, it receives Rs. 6.90 back from  the 
share of Central taxes. Tamil Nadu, one of India’s strongest economic contributors 
and a major generator of national tax revenues, receives only a fraction of what it 
contributes. Meanwhile, several States that contribute far less receive significantl y 
larger transfers. The people of Tamil Nadu deserve a fair and equitable share of the 
resources they help generate. If the principle of cooperative federalism is to have any 
real meaning, the Union Government must address this glaring disparity and ensure  
that the distribution of national resources does not systematically disadvantage high -
performing States like Tamil Nadu or penalise them for the same. If the Union 
Government truly believes in cooperative federalism, it must answer a simple 
question: Why is Tamil Nadu paying Rs. 1 and getting back only 23 paise from Central 
divisible pool of taxes? At the same time, why do other States receive Rs. 1.66 or 
even Rs. 6.90 for every rupee contributed? Tamil Nadu has demonstrated that fiscal 
discipline is not a bout cutting welfare - it is about managing resources intelligently 
and prioritising people.  But, what does the Centre do? It withholds our rightful funds, 
cuts devolution, delays dues, and then turns around to preach fiscal morality. All this 
while, its own debt continues to balloon and its interest burden tightens like a noose 
around its Budget. Several crucial Central scheme funds owed to Tamil Nadu remain 
pending. According to detailed representations made by the Tamil Nadu Government, 
the Union Govern ment owes more than Rs. 10,000 crore across various schemes. 
Sir, take the Jal Jeevan Mission. The Union Government has released only Rs. 5,914 
crore, leaving Rs. 3,100 crore still pending. Madam, whenever you deny our lawful 
dues and give pinpricks throug h Governor, one slogan our leader Shri M.K. Stalin 
always say to our people of Tamil Nadu is   & “Tamil Nadu will fight; Tamil Nadu will 
win.”  
Sir, several funds remain only on paper. Take, for example, urban scheme 
fund announced in Budget, but till today we have not received anything. You may 
control the narrative today, you may withhold our lawful funds amounting to more 
than Rs.10,000 crore, twist data and preach fiscal sermons from Delhi, but there is

SHRI S. NIRANJAN REDDY (Andhra Pradesh): Thank you, Deputy Chairman, Sir, for 
giving me this opportunity to speak here.  Sir, I wonder how much has changed since 
the 1st of February when the Finance Bill was first presented. This should be any 
Budget-maker’s nightmare. Crude and Brent prices have  gone up, fertilizer prices 
have gone up, there is a shortage. Inflation is likely to go up; the dollar price has 
increased. So, Sir, from the time the Budget was presented on the 1st of February, 
there has been a sea change. The only piece of advice I hav e for the Finance Ministry 
is: Please try to control the controllables.  
Sir, while I wish them the best in trying to do this firefighting, we cannot lose 
sight of certain long -term objectives that the Finance Bill normally attends to. Even in 
my Finance B ill speech last year, I had focused on Defence Budget and Defence 
allocation. I am going back to that particular topic of Defence allocation. Sir, Defence 
allocation in the Finance Bill and the appropriations provide for the largest Budget 
head. It provide s for ₹7,84,000 crores, which corresponds to 14.7 per cent of the 
Central expenditure. This is the largest Budget head. But, I was hearing the speech of 
the hon’ble Member, Mr. Wilson. The interest burden we bear is 28 per cent, twice of 
our Defence Budget . The Defence Budget has been increasing in terms of absolute 
numbers. But, you might be surprised to note that, structurally, the Defence Budget 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
99

is still less than 2 per cent of the GDP. Just for us to understand, in 1985, India was 
spending 3.6 per cent of its GDP on Defence. In 1990, it came down to 3.1 per cent. 
In 2000, it came down to 2.9 per cent. It remained steady at 2.9 per cent till 2005. In 
2024, it came down to 1.6 per cent. I can understand, Sir, why this came down over a 
period of time. I agr ee when the hon’ble Prime Minister said that the era of war is 
over. I think, now, the era of peace is over. Let us acknowledge it with the global 
strife and conflicts that are happening. We cannot any more think that the era of war 
is over. We need to be prepared on the Defence side. When you look at world 
examples, Poland today spends 4.48 per cent of GDP on Defence, when it is 
confronted with the crisis of Russia on one border. The entire NATO has pledged that 
they must increase their Defence spending to  5 per cent. Sir, India is very, very 
uniquely placed where we have threats on two sides of our borders.  So, the Budget 
of 2 per cent of GDP is woefully inadequate. Even out of the 2 per cent out of the GDP 
that is set apart for the Defence Budget, 22 per  cent of this 2 per cent goes for 
pensions, another 22 per cent goes for salaries and, with the Eighth Pay Commission 
recommendations likely to come, that is going to increase.  So, out of the two per 
cent of the GDP that is allocated for Defence, the capit al expenditure is just 17 per 
cent and R and D expenditure is 3.7 per cent. We need to increase the Defence 
allocation for our Defence Forces; we need to have more acquisitions; we need to 
have more hardware; and we need to replenish very fast. Sir, I recognize that we have 
many challenges. The Finance Minister will be constrained because she will have 
immediate demands today, where there is going to be an imported crude oil bill that 
will increase tremendously. There will be a fertilizer bill that will inc rease. We may see 
some Supplementary Demands for Grants that may come. Now, amidst all this, she 
would still have to find a way for increasing Defence allocation because in the last four 
years that I have been a Member of Parliament, I have not seen one ye ar when there 
has not been a demand that was compelling for that particular year. Each year, the 
Government will have some compelling interest, some developments.  If we keep 
pushing the Defence allocations for a future date, that future date will never com e.  
Sir, I have one recommendation. I have heard several States complain of the cesses 
that are being collected from the Budget which is not a part of the divisible pool for 
the States that are entitled to get a share. My recommendation for the hon’ble 
Finance Minister is this. Please scrap all cesses that you are collecting towards 
various charges. Have one Defence cess. I do not think any State will really grudge if 
the Defence cess money that is collected is used only for capital expenditure which 
will s teal up our Armed Forces, so long as it is reasonable.  My personal 
recommendation to the Government and to the hon’ble Finance Minister is, please do

SHRI MANAS RANJAN MANGARAJ (Odisha) : Thank you hon’ble Deputy Chairman, 
Sir, for giving me this opportunity.  I rise to speak on the Union Finance Bill with a 
deep sense of concern. This Bill which defines the Government's taxes and priorities 
and fiscal direction once again reflects a pattern of systematic n eglect of Odisha.  
While the Government speaks of inclusivity, the financial provisions tell a different 
story.  
Sir, let me be very clear. Odisha is not asking for charity. Odisha is demanding 
its rightful share. The Government talks about Purvodaya, the development of Eastern 
India. But I ask: Where is Odisha in this vision? When we examine this Finance Bill, 
we find no meaningful fiscal incentives, no targeted policy push, and no serious 
attempt to correct regional imbalance.  
Sir, taxation is not just about collecting revenue. It is also about ensuring fair 
distribution and balanced development. Odisha is one of the largest contributors to 
India's mineral wealth. Coal, iron ore and bauxite from our State fuel the nation's 
industries and contribute significantly to the national exchequer. But what does 
Odisha receive in return? Limited reinvestment, inadequate fiscal support and 
growing regional disparity! This is not ‘cooperative federalism.’ This is ‘extractive 
federalism.’ Sir, we demand a fair revision of mineral royalties and a greater share in 
the revenue generated from our resources. We also demand fiscal incentives that 
promote value addition within Odisha so that industries and jobs are created in our 
State, not shifted elsewhere.  
102
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

Sir, infrastructure remains another area of concern. This Finance Bill does not 
provide adequate tax support or financial push for critical projects in Odisha, whether 
it is railway expansion, freight corridors or inland waterways like National Waterway 5. 
If fiscal policy cannot support infrastructure in a resource -rich State, then what is its 
purpose? In agriculture, the challenges are equally serious.  Farmers in Odisha 
continue to struggle with fertilizer shortages, weak procurement and lack o f market 
access. This Finance Bill fails to provide targeted relief or meaningful incentives to 
strengthen the agriculture sector.  
Sir, there are also important inter-State issues like Mahanadi water dispute and 
Polavaram project which directly affect our  people. The silence of the Centre, both 
policy-wise and financially, is deeply concerning. On tourism, Odisha has immense 
untapped potential. Heritage sites like Lalitgiri, Ratnagiri and Udayagiri can become 
major economic drivers.  But, without fiscal sup port and incentives, this potential 
remains neglected. Sir, this continued neglect must end.  Therefore, I urge the 
Government to ensure fair revenue sharing and revision of mineral royalties, provide 
targeted fiscal incentives for industrialization and inf rastructure, strengthen agriculture 
through direct financial support and policy measures, allocate resources for tourism 
and heritage development and address inter -State issues with urgency and financial 
backing.  
Hon’ble Deputy Chairman, Sir, Odisha has a lways contributed to the nation's 
growth with sincerity and strength. But today, I must say, our presence should not be 
mistaken for acceptance of injustice. We demand fairness, we demand equitable 
fiscal treatment and we demand rightful recognition in Ind ia's development journey. 
Thank you, Sir. Vande Utkal Janani!

Beedha. 
SHRI MASTHAN RAO YADAV BEEDHA (Andhra Pradesh) : Sir, I rise to speak to 
support the Finance Bill, 2026, on behalf of our Telugu Desam Party and our leader, 
Shri Nara Chandrababu Naidu Garu. The Finance Bill, 2026, rests  on five principles. 
One, trust-based tax administration; two, ease of living for common citizens; three, 
empowering the MSMEs, farmers and cooperatives; four, making India a stronger 
global business hub; five, customs reform.  
 First is trust -based tax ad ministration and legal framework. With the foreign 
assets of Small Taxpayers Disclosure Scheme 2026, a new chapter allows small 
taxpayers to declare small overseas assets up to Rs.5 crore or undisclosed foreign 
income upto Rs.1 crore without facing prosecu tion, providing immense relief for 
inadvertent omissions.  Now I come to decriminalization and reduction of 
116
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

punishments. The Bill marks a clear shift away from a ‘penalise first’ approach by 
rationalising criminal provisions under the Income -tax framework. Then revised and 
updated returns. To facilitate compliance, taxpayers are now permitted to update or 
revise returns even after reassessment proceedings, subject to an additional ten per 
cent tax. Additionally, the time limit for revised return is extended to 12 months from 
the end of the relevant tax year. Then I come to Dispute Resolution Committee. The 
DRC empowered to waive off penalties and grant immunity from prosecution for small 
and medium taxpayers, aiming to reduce litigation.  
 Now I come to middle class and ease of living reliefs. First is collection of tax 
at source. TCS for remittances under the Liberalised Remittance Scheme (LRS) for 
education and medical purposes has been slashed from five per cent to two per cent. 
Similarly, TCS on overseas tour packages has been significantly reduced from 20 per 
cent to 2 per cent, while the threshold limit for 20 per cent TCS applicability has been 
completely removed. Tax Deducted at Source – A new system allows certain 
depositors to directly accept declarations like Form 15G and Form 15H and distribute 
them on behalf of senior citizens and small savers, ending the hassle of submitting 
multiple physical forms to different agencies. Exemption for disability pension, a 
statutory tax exemption, has been granted for disability pensions. Then, Amendment 
of First Schedule – Passenger allowances, tariffs on gifts and rules around personal 
imports -- have been rationalised to reduce daily disputes at airports.  Then, 
agriculture, MSMEs and cooperatives: Deduction in respect of income of cooperative 
societies – Primary cooperative societies now enjoy tax deductions not just for 
supplying milk, fruits and vegetables, but the ambit has been expanded to include 
cattle feed and cotton seed. Deduction for I nter-Cooperative Dividends – Dividend 
income received by federal cooperatives from their investments is now tax -
deductable under the new tax regime, provided it is distributed further down to its 
members. Special provision for fishing and fishing related a ctivities – Indian-flagged 
fishing vessels operating beyond India’s territorial waters can now bring harvested fish 
into India completely free of customs duty.  Then, data centres and digital 
infrastructure sector – To prevent foreign cloud providers from s etting up hollow 
operations, tax holidays up to 2047 are now strictly tied to structural conditionalities 
and domestic value creation. The physical infrastructure must be built and operated 
locally and all sales to Indian users must be routed through an In dian reseller entity.  
Sir, here, I want to bring to your kind notice, while the previous Government between 
2019 and 2024, stalled progress, destroyed the State’s economy, but, now, our 
hon’ble Chief Minister, Shri Nara Chandrababu Naidu garu, committed to revive and 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
117

complete pending projects. Sir, this is for your kind information. With these words, I 
support the Finance Bill, 2026. Thank you.

DR. FAUZIA KHAN (Maharashtra) : Mr. Vice -Chairman Sir, in spite of al l my 
admiration for the hon’ble Finance Minister, I want to oppose this Bill in a language of 
concern, because what this Bill does is undermine the very conditions under which 
the country can grow. Before we speak of development, we must ask whether we are 
sustaining the nation. This Bi ll carries retrospective tax amendments through 
provisions like the newly inserted Section 292BC.  It uses the words ‘notwithstanding 
anything contained in any judgement’, overriding court rulings that struck down tax 
orders on procedural grounds. Courts ha ve spoken, Sir, the Bill silences them. For 
years, the 2012 Vodafone Retrospective Tax Bill was condemned as a betrayal of 
investors’ confidence. In 2021, it was corrected. Amendments were made to restore 
certainty and to tell the world that India respects  the finality of law. Today, with this 
Bill, we are walking back that promise. The OECD's tax certainty surveys have 
consistently found that retrospective changes in domestic law are among the top 
concerns for investors in emerging markets. Sir, to take India from rank 142 to rank 63 
on the Ease of Doing Business Index was a decade of effort; I understand that. But, 
the tax unpredictability quietly erases it. When a business cannot price legal certainty 
into its plan, it does not plan. It leaves. Imprisonme nt for several offences has been 
reduced from seven years to two years. For others, the punishment is now only a fine. 
I would ask the House to hold two facts together.  India's central tax -to-GDP ratio 
120
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

stands at around 11 to 12 per cent against an OECD average of over 34 per cent. And, 
there are, as per CBDT data, over 5.8 lakh pending tax litigations, the vast majority 
involving individuals and small businesses, not large corporates. Sir, these are 
ordinary people who face the weight of notices, penalties  and prosecution. 
Meanwhile, this Bill extends leniency to those who have the least excuse for non -
compliance and the most resources to comply. Sir, a nation cannot sustain itself on a 
social contract this unequal.  Household financial  savings have decline d sharply. 
...(Time-bell rings.)... Sir, just give me half-a-minute. Youth unemployment remains 
above 16 per cent. The rupee faces sustained pressure touching record lows against 
the dollar. We are not in a position to absorb the consequences of bad fiscal  policy. 
We cannot afford a Bill that makes investors uncertain and makes the powerful 
comfortable. A Finance Bill is a statement of priorities. It tells this country who the 
State stands behind. And, this Bill does not stand behind the right people. I urg e the 
Government to withdraw these provisions. We cannot build a developed India on a 
foundation we keep shifting beneath our feet.  Thank you, Sir.

THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (DR. SASMIT PATRA): Now, Shrimati Nirmala Sitharamanji to 
give a combined reply to the discussions on the Appropriation (No. 2) Bill, 2026 and 
the Finance Bill, 2026. 
THE MINISTER OF FINANCE ; AND THE MINISTER OF CORPORATE  
AFFAIRS (SHRIMATI NIRMALA SITHARAMAN): Thank you very much, Sir, for giving 
me an opportunity to reply to the discussion on this year's Finance Bill. On the subject 
122
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

of the Budget, since I have already replied, I am taking up the Finance Bill and the 
Appropriation together. 
(MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN in the Chair.) 
The Finance Bill was drafted with the reforms in mind, the ‘reform express’ 
about which the hon’ble Prime Minister keeps referring to. The momentum on reforms 
has not come down at all. The Finance Bill also has elements of these reforms bei ng 
pushed because we want India to be a lot more economically-vibrant country. So, the 
‘reform express’ is what guided us to bring in very many elements into the Finance Bill 
so that it reflects the mood of the nation. The guiding principle has been inclus ivity, 
and we have made sure that every section gets to benefit from an opening economy. 
The approach also has been to look at every law that is there, every regulation or 
notification which guide certain activities, with an intention of making sure that w e 
decriminalize, and the regulation is a soft touch regulation. So, the Finance Bill has 
gone into drafting these kinds of regulations which are very, very onerous and almost 
punishing, and we have tried to bring that to only being a compounding or levying  of 
penalty and so on. So, with this intention, we have also made newer changes which 
have been brought in to, let us say, ease passengers when they are getting out of the 
country or coming in with the kind of things that they bring in. We wanted to give 
them a favourable treatment. So, many provisions of the Customs Act which will be 
impacting on people who are travelling abroad have also been tweaked in favour of 
the passengers. We are also providing for foreign assets disclosure scheme, updation 
of returns even after assessment; reassessment proceedings have commenced and 
also rationalization of passenger elements, which is what I referred to a minute ago. 
Besides this, the Customs Administration has already begun the reform process, 
simplifying the process. In fact, I have said it earlier in this House. In one year, that is, 
2025, the Department of Revenue undertook reforming the Income Tax Act of 1961, 
coming up with a new Act which is simpler, easier to comply with and is also seen in 
the Forms that we are going to use for Income Tax Returns filing. The same year, the 
Department of Revenue in September did one major reform of GST as a result of 
which, today the markets are a lot more buoyant. You are able to see people buying 
things which they had postpo ned earlier because they were awaiting some kind of a 
change in the GST. Although, from the time the GST was launched, till earlier than 
September, 2025, we had already come down from 15.5 to about 11.2 per cent. Post 
this, it has touched 10 per cent, and probably in some items, even less than 10. On an 
average, it is about 10 per cent.  So, you may see that the Department of Revenue 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
123

not only amended the Income Tax Act, but looked at customs, looked at GST, all 
within one year, and a major reshaping of the tax administration has been taken up.  
Sir, for small businesses and artisans, I think it was right that we removed

replacing manual checks with digital, faceless cleara nces to ensure faster, hassle -
free movement of goods. So, I think, overall, the approach has been to make it easier 
for people to do business.  
Sir, without getting into too much detail on the Finance Bill, which the Lok 
Sabha has passed and for which we have come here to take the views of hon’ble 
Members of this House, I want to start responding to some of the observations made 
by hon’ble Members. Sir, though not so much related to the Finance Bill, hon’ble 
Member, Dr. Vikramjit Singh Sahney, who is probably not here now, had commented 
that our systems are getting stuck, we are making plans, but we are not able to 
execute it, what is promised on p aper is not actually happening or delivered on the 
ground, and so on. I just want to draw the attention of the hon’ble Member to the fact 
that we have brought in a Single Nodal Agency system, which is a model for Centrally 
sponsored schemes to improve spen ding efficiency by routing them through a 
dedicated and traceable account structure. So, the fiscal public finance management 
system has gone through a thorough change. It is now a transparent mechanism from 
where the money goes to the particular account h ead in a State Government through 
a process called Single Nodal Account,  SNS-SPARSH.  SPARSH actually ensures 
that money goes ‘Just - in-Time’ to the accounts, the State Government also puts its 
share and, without delay, both together get to the ground. And , therefore, when the 
utilization certificates come back to us, we give the next tranche, and so on. So, no 
project waits for resources to come.  Equally, we do not park money in programs in 
the States, or periodically check up if the money is getting spen t because, after all, 
many of the projects and many of our expenditure is now based on borrowed money.  
We want to borrow just enough to take care of our work and not borrow much more 
and then keep paying interest. So, this SNS -SPARSH, in which all the Sta tes are on 
board, ensures faster release of funds, better visibility for utilization and reduced 
parking of public money. So, this push -based system that we have now brought in 
actually enables funds to go to the last mile and it can be tracked and traced at any 
point in time, both by the Central Government and the local body and also the State 
Governments. So, this ‘just-in-time’ has actually helped us.  Now, we have not left it 
at that. In the Budget documents, there is a statement 4AA of the expenditure profile 
that we produce every time during the Budget, which actually gives a comprehensive 
124
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

State-wise and scheme -wise breakdown of the actual releases that are made of 
funds for the Centrally sponsored schemes. So, it is not just digitally transparent for 
all of us to see, the State Government, Central Government and also the concerned 
department at the State level, but it is also available as a document in every budgetary 
set of documents that we produce for all members to see it. So, this system has 
actually helped us to prudentially use money without wasting it.

Hon’ble Member, Shakti Singh Gohil, raised a lot of quest ions about the new 
standard of the GDP numbers, the new standard to belittle previous numbers so that 
‘my number may look good and, in this series, you have revised the GDP numbers to 
belittle the previous numbers’. These are routine exercises in the Gover nment. I will 
[ 27 March, 2026 ]
125

tell you the number of times Government of India has done this and not randomly; it 
goes systematically.  Nine revisions since Independence have happened. 
Governments of every political persuasion have done this. The first Base Year of 
1948-49 was released in 1956 under Prime Minister Nehru’s Congress Government, 
followed by 1960-61, which was released in 1967 and 1970-71 Base Year was released 
in 1978 under the Janata Party Government under Shri Morarji Desai. Rajiv Gandhi ji’s 
Congress Govern ment released the 1980 -81 Base Year Series in 1988 and Shri 
Vajpayee ji under the NDA Government released the 1993 -94 Series in 1999. Dr. 
Manmohan Singh carried two revisions, releasing the 1999 -2000 series in the year 
2006 and the 2004 -2005 series in 2010 . The current outgoing 2011 -12 series was 
released in 2015 under hon’ble Prime Minister Modi and now the 2022 -23 series has 
already been released. So, this happens in every 10 years. I am sure hon’ble Member, 
Shri Shakti Singh Gohil, is not suggesting that every previous revision was to taunt the 
earlier Governments. No; this is a process of governance. It happens under every 
Government. So, I am sure and I confidently believe, every time it is not for belittling 
the predecessor Governments. They are done b ecause the base has to be 
scientifically tested, statistically proved and then kept on.   
5.00 P.M. 
   In this new series, several new and improved data sources are being used 
to bring in granularity, accuracy and reliability of the estimates. More than 300 data 
sources and about 1,400 variables are all used to make this GADP series, and it 
captures household sector information also because now, it uses the Annual Survey 
of Unincorporated Sector Enterprises, ASUSE, as we say it, and the PLFS, the 
Periodic Labour Force Survey. These are all used for better picture of the economy.  
Also now, we have the adva ntage of the GST data, which shows where companies 
are operating and their output is assigned to the right States accurately. So, the data 
has now become a lot more comprehensive, a lot more vibrant and a lot more true to 
the time in which the data is bein g extracted. The PFMS real -time data actually 
reduces errors. So, we are not coming up with the number which, probably, will need 
a further correction, or in which the margin of error is higher. That is not the case.  
Now, we are able to get even better data.  It avoids delays and makes GDP estimates 
more accurate. We are also using the e -Vahan, rail and air transport services’ data 
and fuel-related data so that we are able to estimate better value for the goods and

observers of the Indian economy. They did not have a reason to worry about it, but I 
want to request the Member to have greater, in-depth look into what has come out.

Concern over devastating fire at Dastkar Nature Bazaar 
SHRIMATI PRIYANKA CHATURVEDI (Maharashtra) : Sir, a devastatin g fire broke out 
at Dastkar’s Nature Bazaar in South Delhi on 16 March 2026, gutting over 40 stalls just 
hours before the market opened for the ongoing “Bonanza Bazaar” crafts exhibition. 
The Dastkar Nature Bazaar, a private non-profit organisation, has been working since 
138
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

1981 to support Indian traditional craftspeople and today works with over 1 lakh 
artisans across rural India. As per the official statement of the orga nizer, goods worth 
more than Rs.5 crores were destroyed, severely impacting artisans from States such 
as West Bengal, Nagaland, Madhya Pradesh, Tamil Nadu, Kerala, Kashmir and 
Rajasthan. While no casualties were reported and the blaze was brought under 
control after over 90 minutes, the remaining days of the exhibition were cancelled, 
depriving artisans of crucial peak-season income.  
 Sir, India has 7 million artisans. They earn  US $ 3.40 to 4.50 daily in cities, 
only 89 cents in villages, which is barely subsistence. Only 34 per cent have 
consistent wages. Despite being the backbone of our rural economy and cultural 
heritage, this sector faces disorganization, limited capital, poor technology access, 
absent market intelligence, and weak institutional frame works. When a single fire 
wipes out a year's earnings for vulnerable artisans, Government’s intervention 
becomes essential. 
I urge the Government to assess losses, provide emergency financial relief to 
compensate the destroyed inventory,  offer rehabilitation support, including 
concessional working capital loans and conduct fire safety audits at all major 
handicraft exhibition venues nationwide. Sir, artisans are the custodians of centuries -
old skills that once lost, cannot be revived.

Concern over removal of Respiratory Medicine (TB and Chest) from the list of

श्री शगिसिंह िोगहल (गुजरात): महोदय, ििर 2023 में राष्रीय नचनकत्सा आयोग (एिएमसी) िे 
एमबीबीएस कॉिेजों क े  निए िए मािक, य जीएमएसआर, 2023 जारी नकए, नजसमें श्िसि रोग 
निभाग (रेल्स्परेटरी मेनडनसि/टीबी एण्ड चेस्ट) को अनििायर निभागों की स ची से हटा नदया गया।  
इसक े  पनरणामस्िरूप िए मेनडकि कॉिेजों में श्िसि रोग निभाग रििा अनििायर िहीं रहा। इससे 
एमबीबीएस छात्रों को टीबी, अस्थमा, सीओपीडी जैसी बीमानरयों का निशेिज्ञ प्रनशक्षण िहीं 
नमिेगा। इि कॉिेज अस्पतािों में मरीजों को पल्मोिोिॉनजस्ट उपिब्ि िहीं होगा। इसी प्रकार, 
आईपीएचएस, 2022 में भी नजिा अस्पतािों क े  निए श्िसि रोग निभाग शानमि िहीं नकया गया। 
भारत की सबसे बड़ी संरामक बीमारी, टीबी और सबसे आम पुरािी बीमानरयों (अस्थमा और 
सीओपीडी) का इिाज करिे िािे निभाग को नशक्षा और सरकारी  अस्पतािों, दोिों से हटा नदया 
गया है।  
 महोदय, श्िसि रोग निभाग को नफर से बहाि करिा इसनिए अनििायर है, क्योंनक 
पहिा, भारत में दुनिया क े  सबसे अनिक टीबी रोगी हैं। जो निभाग टीबी का निदाि और उपचार 
करता है, उसे हटािा माििीय प्रिाि मंत्री जी की टीबी उन्म िि योजिा  (2025 िक्ष्य) को सीिे 
कमजोर करिा है। द सरा, श्िसि रोग (अस्थमा, सीओपीडी , आईएिडी, फ े फड़ों का क ैं सर, 
स्िीप एपनिया) ओपीडी और भती क े  सबसे प्रमुि कारणों में है। इिका उपचार निशेिज्ञ 
नचनकत्सक द्वारा होिा चानहए , सामान्य नचनकत्सक द्वारा िहीं। तीसरा, िए मेनडकि कॉ िेजों में, 
निशेिकर ग्रामीण एिं छोटे शहरों में, श्िसि रोग क े  इिाज की कोई सुनििा िहीं है। ब्रॉन्कोस्कोपी, 
स्पायरोमेरी, एिआईिी जैसी बुनियादी प्रनरयाओं क े  निए मरीजों को सैकड़ों नकिोमीटर द र 
जािा पड़ता है।  
 अत: सरकार से मेरी यह मांग है नक इस पर ध्याि नदया जाए। िन्यिाद।

Now, Shriman Niranjan Bishi.   
Need to strengthen the National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST) 
SHRI NIRANJAN BISHI (Odisha) : Mr. Vice -Chairman, Sir, I seek to draw the 
attention of the Government towards the functioning of the National Commission for 
Scheduled Tribes (NCST), a vital constitutional body entrusted with safeguarding the 
rights and interests of our tribal communities. The NCST has been established under 
Article 338A of the C onstitution of India, with the mandate to investigate, monitor and 
evaluate all matters relating to the safeguards provided for the Scheduled Tribes. The 
Commission is vested with the powers of a Civil Court in matters of inquiry, including 
summoning witne sses, requisitioning documents, and receiving evidence on 
affidavits. As of late 2023 and early 2024, four out of five key constitutional positions in 
the NCST remain vacant — including the Chairperson (vacant since 27 June 2023), 
the Vice -Chairperson (vac ant since 2019), and two Member positions. This has 
severely affected the decision -making capacity and institutional effectiveness of the 
Commission. Further, out of a total sanctioned strength of 124 posts, over  70 
positions remain vacant, including critical posts such as Directors, Research Officers, 
Law Officers, Accounts Officers, and Superintendent of Police. The Commission is 
functioning at less than 50 per cent capacity, significantly hampering its ability to 
conduct investigations, address grievances, and ensure justice for Scheduled Tribes. 
Therefore, I urge the Government to take necessary steps to fill all vacant positions, 
strengthen institutional capacity, and empower the NCST to effectively discharge its 
constitutional responsibilities across the country.                                                 
THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (DR. SASMIT PATRA): Thank you.
